Figure 34.
The
\leq
Relation For PL States

We now define a relation
\leq
between PL states and HLPL states.
Contrary to the other relations in this paper and because the states of PL and HLPL are
quite difference,
\leq
is not defined as a series of transformations between states but
in a more standard manner.

Definition B.1 (Refinement Between PL and HLPL).

For
\Omega^{\text{pl}}
a PL state and
\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}
an HLPL state, we state
that
\Omega^{\text{pl}}
and
\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}
are in relation, noted
\Omega^{\text{pl}}\leq\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}
,
if there exists
\mathsf{blockof}
,
\mathsf{addrof}
such that:

\displaystyle\mathsf{compatible}\;\mathsf{blockof}\;\mathsf{addrof}\;\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}\;\wedge\;\Omega^{\text{pl}}\leq\mathcal{C}_{\Omega}\;{\mathsf{blockof}}\;{\mathsf{addrof}}\;{\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}}

We now turn to the proof of the forward simulation between PL and HLPL.
We want to prove the theorem
B.2
.

Theorem B.2 (Forward Simulation From HLPL to PL).

For all
\Omega^{\text{pl}}
PL state and
\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}
HLPL state we have:

\displaystyle\Omega^{\text{pl}}\leq\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\forall\,s\,r\,\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}_{1},\,\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}\vdash_{\mathsf{hlpl}}\,s\rightsquigarrow(r,\,\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}_{1})\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\exists\,\Omega^{\text{pl}}_{1},\,\Omega^{\text{pl}}\vdash_{\mathsf{pl}}\,s\rightsquigarrow(r,\,\Omega^{\text{pl}}_{1})\;\wedge\;\Omega^{\text{pl}}_{1}\leq\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}_{1}

We need a series of auxiliary lemmas.

The following lemma is straightforward to prove, but provides a crucial property
to make the proofs work for the expressions and assignments, which are the difficult
cases. In particular, it gives us that assignments don’t move or duplicate locations,
meaning the view of locations as addresses remains consistent between the HLPL
state and the PL state.

Lemma B.3 (HLPL-Rvalue-NoLoc).

For all
\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}
HLPL state,
rv
,
v
and
\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}_{1}
such that

\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}\vdash_{\mathsf{hlpl}}\,rv\Downarrow(v,\,\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}_{1})
,
there are no
\mathsf{loc}
values in
v
.

Proof

By induction on
\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}\vdash_{\mathsf{hlpl}}\,rv\Downarrow(v,\,\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}_{1})
.

•

Case
\mathsf{copy}\,p
. Trivial induction on the value we read.
We use the fact that copying a value in HLPL returns
the same value but where the locations have been removed (see
Copy-Loc

in particular).

•

Case
\mathsf{move}\,p
. Trivial by the premises of
HLPL-E-Move
.

•

Case
E-Pointer
(
\mathsf{\&}\,p

\mathsf{\&reserved}\,p
,
\mathsf{\&mut}\,p
).
Trivial by the fact that the value is a pointer.

•

Case
\mathsf{new}\,op
. Trivial by the fact that the value is a pointer.

•

Case constant. Trivial.

•

Case Adt constructor. Trivial.

•

Case unary/binary operations (not, neg, +, -, etc.). Trivial.

Lemma B.4 (HLPL-PL-Read).

For all
\Omega^{\text{pl}}
PL state and
\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}
HLPL state,
\mathsf{blockof}
,

\mathsf{addrof}
we have:

\displaystyle\mathsf{compatible}\;\mathsf{blockof}\;\mathsf{addrof}\;\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\Omega^{\text{pl}}\leq\mathcal{C}_{\Omega}\;{\mathsf{blockof}}\;{\mathsf{addrof}}\;{\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}}\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\forall\,p\,k\,v\,\tau,\,(\vdash\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}(p)\overset{\mathsf{k}}{\Rightarrow}v:\tau)\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\exists\,\vec{v},\,\vdash\Omega^{\text{pl}}(p:\tau)\overset{\mathsf{k}}{\Rightarrow}[\vec{v}]\;\wedge\;[\vec{v}]\leq\mathcal{C}_{v}\;{\mathsf{blockof}}\;{\mathsf{addrof}}\;{v}

Proof

Straightforward by induction on
p
.

Lemma B.5 (Rvalue-Preserves-PL-HLPL-Rel).

For all
\Omega^{\text{pl}}
PL state and
\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}
HLPL state,
\mathsf{blockof}
,

\mathsf{addrof}
we have:

\displaystyle\mathsf{compatible}\;\mathsf{blockof}\;\mathsf{addrof}\;\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\Omega^{\text{pl}}\leq\mathcal{C}_{\Omega}\;{\mathsf{blockof}}\;{\mathsf{addrof}}\;{\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}}\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\forall\;rv\,v\,\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}_{1},\,\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}\vdash_{\mathsf{hlpl}}\,rv\Downarrow(v,\,\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}_{1})\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\exists\;\mathsf{blockof}_{1}\;\mathsf{addrof}_{1}\;\vec{v},\,

\displaystyle\quad\Omega^{\text{pl}}\vdash_{\mathsf{pl}}\,rv\Downarrow\vec{v}\;\wedge\;

\displaystyle\quad\mathsf{compatible}\;{\mathsf{blockof}_{1}}\;{\mathsf{addrof}_{1}}\;{\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}_{1}}\;\wedge\;

\displaystyle\quad\Omega^{\text{pl}}_{1}\leq\mathcal{C}_{\Omega}\;{\mathsf{blockof}_{1}}\;{\mathsf{addrof}_{1}}\;{\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}_{1}}\;\wedge\;

\displaystyle\quad\vec{v}\leq\mathcal{C}_{v}\;{\mathsf{blockof}_{1}}\;{\mathsf{addrof}_{1}}\;{v}

Proof

By induction on
\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}\vdash_{\mathsf{hlpl}}\,rv\Downarrow(v,\,\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}_{1})
.

•

Case
\mathsf{copy}\,p
. We use Lemma
B.4
and the fact

E-Copy
leaves the state unchanged.

•

Case
\mathsf{move}\,p
.
We use Lemma
B.4
to relate the values we evaluate to.
Relating the updated HLPL state to the PL state is slightly more technical.
The goal is implied by the following auxiliary lemma (the proof is straightforward by
induction on the path
P
):

\displaystyle\forall\,P\,v_{0}\,v_{1}\,\tau\,\overrightarrow{v_{0}}\,\overrightarrow{v_{1}}\,\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}_{1},

\displaystyle\mathsf{compatible}\;\mathsf{blockof}\;\mathsf{addrof}\;\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}\vdash P(v_{0})\overset{\mathsf{mov}}{\Rightarrow}v_{1}:\tau\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\text{no location}\in v_{1}\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\Omega^{\text{pl}}\vdash P([\overrightarrow{v_{0}}])\Rightarrow[\overrightarrow{v_{1}}]:\tau\Rightarrow

\displaystyle[\overrightarrow{v_{1}}]\leq\mathcal{C}_{v}\;{\mathsf{blockof}}\;{\mathsf{addrof}}\;{v_{1}}\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}\vdash P(v_{0})\leftarrow(\bot:\tau)\overset{\mathsf{mov}}{\Rightarrow}(v^{\prime}_{1},\,\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}_{1})\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\Omega^{\text{pl}}\leq\mathcal{C}_{\Omega}\;{\mathsf{blockof}}\;{\mathsf{addrof}}\;{\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}_{1}}\;\wedge\;[\overrightarrow{v_{1}}]\leq\mathcal{C}_{v}\;{\mathsf{blockof}}\;{\mathsf{addrof}}\;{v^{\prime}_{1}}

•

Case
E-Pointer
(
\mathsf{\&}\,p

\mathsf{\&reserved}\,p
,
\mathsf{\&mut}\,p
).
Similar to the move case, but if we insert a fresh location, we have to update the

\mathsf{addrof}
function to insert a mapping for the fresh location identifier.

•

Case
\mathsf{new}\,op
. We use the induction hypothesis, and need to insert a binding
in
\mathsf{blockof}
for the fresh box.

•

Case constant. Trivial.

•

Case Adt constructor. Straightforward by using the induction hypotheses
and the rules to evaluate and concretize Adts.

•

Case unary/binary operations (
\neg
, +, -, etc.). Trivial.

Lemma B.6 (Assign-Preserves-PL-HLPL-Rel).

For all
\Omega^{\text{pl}}
PL state and
\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}
HLPL state we have:

\displaystyle\Omega^{\text{pl}}\leq\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\forall\;p\,rv\,\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}_{1},\,\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}\vdash_{\mathsf{hlpl}}\,p:=rv\rightsquigarrow((),\,\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}_{1})\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\exists\;\Omega^{\text{pl}}_{1},\,\Omega^{\text{pl}}\vdash_{\mathsf{pl}}\,p:=rv\rightsquigarrow((),\,\Omega^{\text{pl}}_{1})\;\wedge\;\Omega^{\text{pl}}_{1}\leq\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}_{1}

Proof

We do the proof by induction on
p
. It is very similar to the move case
of Lemma
B.5
. In particular, we use the fact that
there are no locations in
the value we move
(by Lemma
B.3
),
and in the value we overwrite and gets saved to an anonymous value (by the
premises of
HLPL-E-Assign
)

2
2

2

The fact that we need the overwritten value in HLPL to not contain
locations to be able to this proof
(otherwise we break the relation between the locations in the HLPL state and
the addresses in the PL state) is the reason why we ultimately need to forbid
overwriting values which contain outer loans in LLBC (see
E-Move
).
.

Lemma B.7 (Reorg-Preserves-PL-HLPL-Rel).

For all
\Omega^{\text{pl}}
PL state and
\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}
HLPL state we have:

\displaystyle\Omega^{\text{pl}}\leq\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}\Rightarrow\forall\;\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}_{1},\,\Omega^{\text{pl}}\hookrightarrow\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}\Rightarrow\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}\leq\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}_{1}

Proof

By induction on
\Omega^{\text{pl}}\hookrightarrow\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}
.
By
\Omega^{\text{pl}}\leq\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}
there exists
\mathsf{blockof}
,
\mathsf{addrof}
such
that:

\displaystyle\mathsf{compatible}\;\mathsf{blockof}\;\mathsf{addrof}\;\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}\;\wedge\;\Omega^{\text{pl}}\leq\mathcal{C}_{\Omega}\;{\mathsf{blockof}}\;{\mathsf{addrof}}\;{\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}}

•

Case
Reorg-None
. Trivial.

•

Case
Reorg-Seq
. Trivial by the induction hypotheses.

•

Case
Reorg-End-Pointer
.
There exists
\Omega[.]
,
\ell
such that:

\displaystyle\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}=\Omega[\mathsf{ptr}\,\ell]

\displaystyle\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}_{1}=\Omega[\bot]

We have to show that for all
bi
box identifier we have:

\displaystyle\Omega^{\text{pl}}.\mathsf{mem}\;bi\leq\mathcal{C}_{v}\;{\mathsf{blockof}}\;{\mathsf{addrof}}\;{(\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}_{1}.\mathsf{mem}\;(\mathsf{blockof}^{-1}\,{bi}))}

Let’s pose:

\displaystyle[\vec{v}]:=\Omega^{\text{pl}}.\mathsf{mem}\;bi

\displaystyle v_{0}=\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}_{0}.\mathsf{mem}\;(\mathsf{blockof}^{-1}\,{bi})

\displaystyle v_{1}=\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}_{1}.\mathsf{mem}\;(\mathsf{blockof}^{-1}\,{bi})

We have
[\vec{v}]\leq\mathcal{C}_{v}\;{\mathsf{blockof}}\;{\mathsf{addrof}}\;{v_{0}}
and we want to show
[\vec{v}]\leq\mathcal{C}_{v}\;{\mathsf{blockof}}\;{\mathsf{addrof}}\;{v_{1}}
.

There are two cases, depending on whether the hole is in
v_{1}
or not.
More formally, either we have
v_{1}=v_{0}
, in which case the proof is trivial,
or there exists
V[.]
such that
v_{0}=V[\mathsf{ptr}\,\ell]
and

v_{1}=V[\bot]
.

The end of the proof is implied by the following auxiliary theorem, which is straightforward to
prove by induction on
V[.]
:

\displaystyle[\vec{v}]\leq\mathcal{C}_{v}\;{\mathsf{blockof}}\;{\mathsf{addrof}}\;{v_{0}}\Rightarrow

\displaystyle v_{0}=V[\mathsf{ptr}\,\ell]\Rightarrow v_{1}=V[\bot]\Rightarrow

\displaystyle[\vec{v}]\leq\mathcal{C}_{v}\;{\mathsf{blockof}}\;{\mathsf{addrof}}\;{v_{1}}

•

Case
Reorg-End-Loc
.
Similar to above, but this time we have to update
\mathsf{addrof}

to remove the location identifier that we eliminate.

There exists
\Omega[.]
,
\ell
,
v_{s}
such that:

\displaystyle\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}=\Omega[\mathsf{loan}^{s}\,\ell\,v_{s}]

\displaystyle\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}_{1}=\Omega[v_{s}]

Let’s pose
\mathsf{addrof}_{1}:=\mathsf{addrof}_{|(\mathsf{domain}\;\mathsf{addrof})\setminus\{\ell\}}

the restriction of
\mathsf{addrof}
to its domain from which we removed
\ell
.
We show that:

(4)

\displaystyle\Omega^{\text{pl}}\leq\mathcal{C}_{\Omega}\;{\mathsf{blockof}}\;{\mathsf{addrof}_{1}}\;{\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}_{1}}\;\wedge\;

(5)

\displaystyle\mathsf{compatible}\;{\mathsf{blockof}}\;{\mathsf{addrof}_{1}}\;{\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}_{1}}

We show (1) the same way as in the
Reorg-End-Pointer
case.
For (2), the difficult part is the last two conjuncts:

(6)

\displaystyle(\forall\;addr\;\ell\;v,\;(\mathsf{blockof},\,\mathsf{addrof}\vdash\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}_{1}(addr)\Rightarrow\mathsf{loc}\,\ell\,v)\Rightarrow\mathsf{addrof}\,{\ell}=addr)\;\wedge\;

(7)

\displaystyle(\forall\;\ell\in\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}_{1},\;\exists\;addr\;v,\;\mathsf{blockof},\,\mathsf{addrof}\vdash\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}_{1}(addr)\Rightarrow\mathsf{loc}\,\ell\,v)

For (1), we pose
(bi,\,n)
an address and do the proof by induction
on
\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}_{1}.\mathsf{mem}\;bi
(straightforward).
For (2), the compatibility assumption for
\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}
gives us a candidate
address
(bi,\,n)
, and we conclude the proof also by induction on

\Omega^{\text{hlpl}}_{1}.\mathsf{mem}\;bi
.

We can finally turn to the proof of the target theorem (
B.2
).

•

Case empty statement. Trivial.

•

Case
E-Reorg
. By Lemma
B.7
.

•

Case
s_{0};\;s_{1}
. Trivial by the induction hypotheses.

•

Case
p:=rv
. By Lemma
B.6
.

•

Case
if␣then␣else
. Trivial by the induction hypotheses.

•

Case
match
. Trivial by the induction hypotheses.

•

Case
\mathsf{free}\,p
. We remove one block from the map and end the corresponding
pointer: straightforward.

•

Case
\mathsf{return}
. Trivial.

•

Cases
\mathsf{panic}
,
\mathsf{break}\,i
,
\mathsf{continue}\,i
. Trivial.

•

Case loop. Trivial by the induction hypotheses.

•

Case function call.
Similar to the same case in the proof of
Eval-Preserves-HLPL+-Rel
.
We leverage the fact that
PL-E-Call
and
E-Call
have a very
similar structure.
We use the Lemma
B.5
to relate the states resulting
from evaluating the operands given as inputs to the function (trivial induction
on the number of inputs).
We easily get that the states resulting from
\vdash\mathsf{push\_stack}\;
are
related (again, induction on the number of inputs then on the number of
local variables which are not used as inputs; we have to take care to
extend the map
\mathsf{blockof}
to account for the blocks freshly allocated
for the local variables).
The induction hypothesis gives us that the states are related after evaluation
of
body
. If the tag is
\mathsf{panic}
we are done.
If it is
\mathsf{return}
, we prove that the states resulting from
\vdash\mathsf{pop\_stack}\;

are related (induction on the number of local variables, and we use

B.6
for the assignments which “drop” the local variables;
this time we have to remove the blocks which were allocated for the local variables
from the map
\mathsf{blockof}
).
Finally, we do a reasoning similar to the one we did in

B.6
to show that the states are still related after
assigning to the destination.

0.83333pt\textsc{Le-Reborrow-SharedBorrow}\par\frac{}{\Omega,\;A\cup\{\;(v_{0},\;v_{1})\;\}\leq\Omega,\;A\cup\{\;v_{0},\;v_{1}\;\}}\hskip 0.83333pt\textsc{Le-Abs-DeconstructPair}\par\frac{C\in\{\mathsf{Left},\;\mathsf{Right}\}}{\Omega,\;A\cup\{\;C\;v\;\}\leq\Omega,\;A\cup\{\;v\;\}}\hskip 0.83333pt\textsc{Le-Abs-DeconstructSum}\par\frac{\text{ no symbolic values, borrows, loans}\in v}{\Omega\leq\Omega,\,\_\rightarrow\bot}\hskip 0.83333pt\textsc{Le-AnonValue}\par\par\par\par\par\par\par

Figure 37.
The Relation
\leq
about
\text{LLBC}^{+}
States

\frac{\text{no borrows, loans}\in v}{\vdash v\prec^{\text{to-abs}}\emptyset}\hskip 0.83333pt\textsc{ToAbs-Empty}\par\frac{v=\mathsf{Left}\;v^{\prime}\vee v=\mathsf{Right}\;v^{\prime}\hskip 0.83333pt\vdash v^{\prime}\prec^{\text{to-abs}}\overrightarrow{A}}{\vdash v\prec^{\text{to-abs}}\overrightarrow{A}}\hskip 0.83333pt\textsc{ToAbs-Sum}\par\frac{\vdash v\prec^{\text{to-abs}}\overrightarrow{A}}{\vdash\mathsf{Box}\,v\prec^{\text{to-abs}}\overrightarrow{A}}\hskip 0.83333pt\textsc{ToAbs-Box}\par\frac{A\text{ fresh}}{\vdash\mathsf{borrow}^{s}\,\ell\prec^{\text{to-abs}}A\{\mathsf{borrow}^{s}\,\ell\}}\hskip 0.83333pt\textsc{ToAbs-SharedBorrow}\par\frac{A\text{ fresh}}{\vdash\mathsf{loan}^{s}\,\overrightarrow{\ell}\,v\prec^{\text{to-abs}}A\{\mathsf{loan}^{s}\,\overrightarrow{\ell}\,v\}}\hskip 0.83333pt\textsc{ToAbs-SharedLoan}\par\frac{\text{ no borrows, $\bot$}\in v\hskip 0.83333ptv\prec^{\text{to-abs}}\overrightarrow{A}}{\vdash\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell\;v\prec^{\text{to-abs}}(\cup\overrightarrow{A})\cup\{\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell\;\_\}}\hskip 0.83333pt\textsc{ToAbs-MutBorrow}\par\frac{A\text{ fresh}}{\vdash\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell\prec^{\text{to-abs}}A\{\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell\}}\hskip 0.83333pt\textsc{ToAbs-MutLoan}\par\frac{\vdash v_{l}\prec^{\text{to-abs}}\overrightarrow{A}_{l}\hskip 0.83333pt\vdash v_{r}\prec^{\text{to-abs}}\overrightarrow{A}_{r}}{\vdash(v_{l},v_{r})\prec^{\text{to-abs}}\overrightarrow{A}_{l},\;\overrightarrow{A}_{r}}\hskip 0.83333pt\textsc{ToAbs-Pair}

Derived Rules for the
\leq

\text{LLBC}^{+}

C.1.
Forward Simulation for
\text{LLBC}^{+}

We now turn to the proof that evaluation for
\text{LLBC}^{+}
preserves the relation

\leq
over
\text{LLBC}^{+}
states. We need several auxiliary lemmas.

Lemma C.1 (Rvalue-Preserves-LLBC+-Rel).

For all
\Omega_{l}
and
\Omega_{r}

\text{LLBC}^{+}
states and
rv
right-value we have:

\displaystyle\Omega_{l}\leq\Omega_{r}\Rightarrow\forall\;v_{r}\,\Omega^{\prime}_{r},\ \Omega_{r}\vdash_{\mathsf{llbc+}}rv\Downarrow(v_{r},\,\Omega^{\prime}_{r})\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\exists\;v_{l}\,\Omega^{\prime}_{l},\ \Omega_{l}\vdash_{\mathsf{llbc+}}rv\Downarrow(v_{l},\,\Omega^{\prime}_{l})\wedge(v_{l},\,\Omega^{\prime}_{l})\leq(v_{r},\,\Omega^{\prime}_{r})

where we define
(v_{l},\,\Omega^{\prime}_{l})\leq(v_{r},\,\Omega^{\prime}_{r})
as:

\displaystyle(v_{l},\,\Omega^{\prime}_{l})\leq(v_{r},\,\Omega^{\prime}_{r}):=(\Omega^{\prime}_{l},\,\_\rightarrow v_{l})\leq(\Omega^{\prime}_{r},\,\_\rightarrow v_{r})

Proof

By induction on
\Omega^{\prime}_{r},\ \Omega_{r}\vdash_{\mathsf{llbc+}}rv\Downarrow(v_{r},\,\Omega^{\prime}_{r})
.

•

Case
\mathsf{copy}\,p
. By induction on
\Omega_{l}\leq\Omega_{r}
.

–

Case
Le-ToSymbolic
. The interesting case happens if in the right state we
copy the symbolic value we just introduced. If this happens, we use the fact that
copying a symbolic value introduces a fresh symbolic value (
Copy-Symbolic
)
and apply
Le-ToSymbolic
twice to relate the states (once for the original
value, once for the copied value).

–

Case
Le-MoveValue
. We note that moving a value can only
constrain the places we can directly access in the right state.
It also doesn’t have any effect on the values we indirectly access through
shared borrows (thanks to the premise that the hole is not inside a shared
loan).
Following this intuition, we prove by induction on
p
that
\mathsf{copy}\,p
reduces
to the same value in the left state and the right state, and leaves the states
unchanged; we conclude by
using
Le-MoveValue
.

–

Case
Le-Fresh-MutLoan
. Similar to
Le-MoveValue
.
We prove by induction on
p
that
\mathsf{copy}\,p
reduces to the same value in the left state
and the right state, and leaves the states unchanged; we conclude by
Le-Fresh-MutLoan
.

–

Case
Le-Fresh-SharedLoan
. Similar to above. Here, the copy actually succeeds
in the right state if and only if it succeeds in the left state, and it yields
the same value.

–

Case
Le-Reborrow-MutBorrow
. Similar to case
Le-Fresh-SharedLoan
.

–

Case
Le-Fresh-SharedBorrow
. Similar to case
Le-Fresh-SharedLoan
.

–

Case
Le-Reborrow-SharedLoan
. We might use a shared borrow to copy
the fresh symbolic value in the right state and the original value in the
left state. Similarly to the case
Le-ToSymbolic
, we use the fact that

Copy-Symbolic
introduces a fresh symbolic value and conclude by using

Le-ToSymbolic
twice if it happens.

–

Case
Le-Abs-End-SharedLoan
. The shared loan in the region abstraction
is not accessible from the outside because there are no remanining borrows pointing
to this value. The copied value is the same in both states, and we conclude
by
Le-Abs-End-SharedLoan
.

–

Case
Le-Abs-End-DupSharedBorrow
. Ending a shared borrow in a region
abstraction doesn’t have any effect on the
\vdash\mathsf{copy}\,p
judgement.
We conclude by
Le-Abs-End-DupSharedBorrow
.

–

Case
Le-Reborrow-SharedBorrow
. Similar to the case
Le-Reborrow-SharedLoan
.

–

Case
Le-ToAbs
. We note that the shared loans, which are accessible from
the non-anonymous values (and so through the copy) are preserved by the
\prec^{\text{to-abs}}

rules (in particular,
ToAbs-SharedLoan
).
We prove by induction on the value that:

\forall\;\mathsf{loan}^{s}\,\ell\,v\in\Omega_{l},\mathsf{loan}^{s}\,\ell\,v\in\Omega_{r}
.
This allows us to prove that the copy operation reduces to the same values
in the left and right states, and we conclude by
Le-ToAbs
.

–

Case
Le-MergeAbs
. Similar to
Le-Abs
: the shared loans
are preserved (by induction on the
\Join
derivation).

–

Cases
Le-ClearAbs
,
Le-Abs-ClearValue
,

Le-Abs-DeconstructPair
,
Le-Abs-DeconstructSum
.
Similar to
Le-ToAbs
: the shared loans are preserved.

–

Case
Le-AnonValue
. Similar to above.

•

Case
\mathsf{move}\,p
. By induction on
\Omega_{l}\leq\Omega_{r}
.

–

Case
Le-ToSymbolic
. Case disjunction on wether we move the symbolic
value or not. We conclude by
Le-ToSymbolic
.

–

Case
Le-MoveValue
. By induction on
p
, we get that
\mathsf{move}\,

reduces to the same value in both states, and yields two states related by
Le-MoveValue
.

–

Case
Le-Fresh-MutLoan
. Similar to
Le-MoveValue
.

–

Case
Le-Fresh-SharedLoan
. Similar to
Le-MoveValue
.

–

Case
Le-Reborrow-MutBorrow
. Similar to
Le-MoveValue
.

–

Case
Le-Fresh-SharedBorrow
. Similar to
Le-MoveValue
.

–

Case
Le-Reborrow-SharedLoan
. Similar to
Le-MoveValue
.
We use the fact that we can’t move a loaned value (
R-SharedLoan
,
W-SharedLoan
)
to show that
\mathsf{move}\,p
reduces to the same value in both states.

–

Case
Le-Abs-End-SharedLoan
. Similar to the case
Le-Reborrow-SharedLoan
.

–

Case
Le-Abs-End-DupSharedBorrow
. Similar to
Le-MoveValue
(and simpler).

–

Case
Le-Reborrow-SharedBorrow
.
Similar to
Le-Reborrow-SharedLoan
, but here we use the fact that we can’t
dereference shared borrows when moving values.

–

Case
Le-ToAbs
.
We note that we can not dereference shared borrows when moving values; this
forbids us from jumping to anonymous values or region abstractions.

–

Case
Le-MergeAbs
.
Same as
Le-ToAbs
.

–

Cases
Le-ClearAbs
,
Le-Abs-ClearValue
,

Le-Abs-DeconstructPair
,
Le-Abs-DeconstructSum
.
Same as
Le-ToAbs
.

–

Case
Le-AnonValue
. Similar to above.

•

Cases
\mathsf{\&}\,p
,
\mathsf{\&reserved}\,
. By induction on
\Omega_{l}\leq\Omega_{r}
.

–

Case
Le-ToSymbolic
. Case disjunction on wether we borrow the symbolic
value or not (we might insert a shared borrow). We conclude by
Le-ToSymbolic
.

–

Case
Le-MoveValue
.
By induction on
p
, we get that
\mathsf{move}\,

reduces to the same value in both states,
and yields two states related by
Le-MoveValue
(we might insert a shared
loan in the moved value).

–

Case
Le-Fresh-MutLoan
. Similar to
Le-MoveValue
.

–

Case
Le-Fresh-SharedLoan
. Similar to
Le-MoveValue
.

–

Case
Le-Reborrow-MutBorrow
. Similar to
Le-MoveValue
.

–

Case
Le-Fresh-SharedBorrow
. Similar to
Le-MoveValue
.

–

Case
Le-Reborrow-SharedLoan
.
Similar to
Le-MoveValue
and

Le-ToSymbolic
(we might borrow the fresh symbolic value or one of the
shared values which were moved to the region abstraction; in the first
case we have to convert one more shared borrow).

–

Case
Le-Abs-End-SharedLoan
. The shared loan inside the region abstraction
is not accessible as there are no remaining borrows to this value.

–

Case
Le-Abs-End-DupSharedBorrow
. This has no impact on the evaluation.

–

Case
Le-Reborrow-SharedBorrow
.
Similar to
Le-Reborrow-SharedLoan
.

–

Case
Le-ToAbs
.
Similar to the
\mathsf{copy}\,p
case: we use the fact that all the shared loans are preserved
in the region abstraction.

–

Case
Le-MergeAbs
.
Same as
Le-ToAbs
.

–

Cases
Le-ClearAbs
,
Le-Abs-ClearValue
,

Le-Abs-DeconstructPair
,
Le-Abs-DeconstructSum
.
Same as
Le-ToAbs
.

–

Case
Le-AnonValue
. Similar to above.

•

Case
\mathsf{\&mut}\,p
. By induction on
\Omega_{l}\leq\Omega_{r}
.
This is similar to the
\mathsf{move}\,p
case, in particular because we can’t mutably
borrow shared values.

–

Case
Le-ToSymbolic
. Case disjunction on wether we mutably borrow the symbolic
value or not. We conclude by
Le-ToSymbolic
.

–

Case
Le-MoveValue
. By induction on
p
, we get that
\mathsf{\&}\,p

reduces to the same value in both states, and yields two states related by
Le-MoveValue
.

–

Case
Le-Fresh-MutLoan
. Similar to
Le-MoveValue
.

–

Case
Le-Fresh-SharedLoan
. Similar to
Le-MoveValue
.

–

Case
Le-Reborrow-MutBorrow
. Similar to
Le-MoveValue
.

–

Case
Le-Fresh-SharedBorrow
. Similar to the move case.

–

Case
Le-Reborrow-SharedLoan
. Similar to the move case.

–

Case
Le-Abs-End-SharedLoan
. Similar to the move case.

–

Case
Le-Abs-End-DupSharedBorrow
. Similar to the move case.

–

Case
Le-Reborrow-SharedBorrow
. Similar to the move case.

–

Case
Le-ToAbs
. Similar to the move case.

–

Case
Le-MergeAbs
. Similar to the move case.

–

Cases
Le-ClearAbs
,
Le-Abs-ClearValue
,

Le-Abs-DeconstructPair
,
Le-Abs-DeconstructSum
.
Similar to the move case.

–

Case
Le-AnonValue
. Similar to above.

•

Case
\mathsf{new}\,op
. We use the induction hypothesis.

•

Case constant. Trivial: the states have no impact on the reduction
of the constants, and are left unchanged.

•

Case Adt constructor. We use the induction hypothesis (the proof is
similar to the
\mathsf{new}\,op
) case.

•

Case unary/binary operations (
\neg
, neg, +, -, etc.). Trivial by
the induction hypotheses.

We now prove the following lemma about assignments.

Lemma C.2 (Assign-Preserves-LLBC+-Rel).

For all
\Omega_{l}
and
\Omega_{r}

\text{LLBC}^{+}
states,
rv
right-value and
p
place we have:

\displaystyle\Omega_{l}\leq\Omega_{r}\Rightarrow\forall\,\Omega^{\prime}_{r},\ \Omega_{r}\vdash_{\mathsf{llbc+}}p:=rv\rightsquigarrow((),\,\Omega^{\prime}_{r})\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\exists\,\Omega^{\prime}_{l}\,\ \Omega_{l}\vdash_{\mathsf{llbc+}}p:=rv\rightsquigarrow((),\,\Omega^{\prime}_{l})\wedge\Omega^{\prime}_{l}\leq\Omega^{\prime}_{r}

Proof

Lemma
C.1
gives us that there exists
v_{r}
,
\Omega^{\prime\prime}_{r}
,

v_{l}
,
\Omega^{\prime\prime}_{l}
such that:

\displaystyle\Omega_{r}\vdash_{\mathsf{llbc+}}rv\Downarrow(v_{r},\,\Omega^{\prime\prime}_{r})\;\wedge\;

\displaystyle\Omega_{l}\vdash_{\mathsf{llbc+}}rv\Downarrow(v_{l},\,\Omega^{\prime\prime}_{l})\;\wedge\;

\displaystyle(v_{l},\,\Omega^{\prime\prime}_{l})\leq(v_{r},\,\Omega^{\prime\prime}_{r})

We do the proof by induction on
(v_{l},\,\Omega^{\prime\prime}_{l})\leq(v_{r},\,\Omega^{\prime\prime}_{r})
, then
on the path
p
. The reasoning is very similar to what we saw in the proof of lemma

C.1
. Something important to note is that the value that gets
overwritten is always saved in a fresh (ghost) anonymous value; this allows us
to conclude in most situations.

•

Case
Le-ToSymbolic
. We have to consider several possibilities:
1. the symbolic value is in the value we move; 2. the symbolic value gets moved to an anonymous value because
of the assignment. We conclude by
Le-ToSymbolic
.

•

Case
Le-MoveValue
. Similar to above.

•

Case
Le-Fresh-MutLoan
. Similar to above.

•

Case
Le-Fresh-SharedLoan
. Similar to above.

•

Case
Le-Reborrow-MutBorrow
. Similar to above.

•

Case
Le-Fresh-SharedBorrow
. Similar to above.

•

Case
Le-Reborrow-SharedLoan
. Similar to above.

•

Case
Le-Abs-End-SharedLoan
. Modifications in region abstractions
have no impact on writes with the move capability.

•

Case
Le-Abs-End-DupSharedBorrow
. Similar to above.

•

Case
Le-Reborrow-SharedBorrow
. Similar to above.

•

Case
Le-ToAbs
. We can’t use a move capability to write to an anonymous value,
so we can conclude by
Le-ToAbs
.

•

Case
Le-MergeAbs
. Similar to above.

•

Cases
Le-ClearAbs
,
Le-Abs-ClearValue
,

Le-Abs-DeconstructPair
,
Le-Abs-DeconstructSum
. Similar to above.

•

Case
Le-AnonValue
. Similar to above.

We now prove that reorganizations preserve the relation between
\text{HLPL}^{+}
states.

Lemma C.3 (Reorg-Preserves-LLBC+-Rel).

For all
\Omega_{l}
and
\Omega_{r}

\text{HLPL}^{+}
states we have:

\displaystyle\Omega_{l}\leq\Omega_{r}\Rightarrow\forall\;\Omega^{\prime}_{r},\ \Omega_{r}\hookrightarrow\Omega^{\prime}_{r}\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\exists\;\Omega^{\prime}_{l},\ \Omega_{l}\hookrightarrow\Omega^{\prime}_{l}\wedge\Omega^{\prime}_{l}\leq\Omega^{\prime}_{r}

Proof

By induction on
\Omega_{r}\hookrightarrow\Omega^{\prime}_{r}
.

•

Case
Reorg-None
. Trivial.

•

Case
Reorg-Seq
. Trivial by the induction hypotheses.

•

Case
Reorg-End-MutBorrow
. By induction on
\Omega_{l}\leq\Omega_{r}
.

–

Case
Le-ToSymbolic
. Case disjunction on whether the symbolic
value is inside the mutably borrowed value or not. We conclude by

Le-ToSymbolic
.

–

Case
Le-MoveValue
. Similar. An important point is that the moved
value can’t come from a region abstraction (this is important because
we can’t end borrows which are inside region abstractions: we have to
end the abstraction first).

–

Case
Le-Fresh-MutLoan
. Similar.

–

Case
Le-Fresh-SharedLoan
. Similar.

–

Case
Le-Reborrow-MutBorrow
.
We have to make a case disjunction on whether we end the fresh borrow
\ell_{1}

or not. If no, we conclude by
Le-Reborrow-MutBorrow
.
If yes, the premises of
Reorg-End-MutBorrow
give us that
the hole is not inside a shared loan, and there are no loans in v.
This allows us to conclude by using
Le-MoveValue
.

–

Case
Le-Fresh-SharedBorrow
. We conclude by
Le-Fresh-SharedBorrow
.

–

Case
Le-Reborrow-SharedLoan
. We conclude by
Le-Reborrow-SharedLoan
.

–

Case
Le-Abs-End-SharedLoan
. We conclude by
Le-Abs-End-SharedLoan
.

–

Case
Le-Abs-End-DupSharedBorrow
. We conclude by
Le-Abs-End-DupSharedBorrow
.

–

Case
Le-Reborrow-SharedBorrow
. We conclude by
Le-Abs-End-DupSharedBorrow
.

–

Case
Le-ToAbs
. We can not end borrows in region abstractions with

Reorg-End-MutBorrow
(we have to use
Reorg-End-Abstraction
).
However, we can end a mutable loan inside a region abstraction.
If the mutable loan is not inside the region abstraction we just introduced,
we conclude by
Le-ToAbs
.
Otherwise, we prove that ending the same mutable loan on the left (it must
be in the anonymous variable we convert to an abstraction) then
converting the anonymous variables to a region abstraction by
Le-ToAbs

yields the same state as on the right (the proof is by induction on
the value we convert).

–

Case
Le-MergeAbs
. Similar to
Le-ToAbs
. The difficult
case is
MergeAbs-Mut
; the important point to notice is that it makes
borrows and loans disappear in the
right
state, meaning there are
more borrows and loans in the left state (this is important, because we
don’t have a well-formedness assumption). In particular, if the mutable
loan we end is in the merged abstraction, then it is also present in
one of the two initial abstractions in the left state, and the corresponding
borrow is not inside a region abstraction.

–

Cases
Le-ClearAbs
,
Le-Abs-ClearValue
,

Le-Abs-DeconstructPair
,
Le-Abs-DeconstructSum
. Trivial.

–

Case
Le-AnonValue
. Trivial.

•

Case
Reorg-End-SharedReservedBorrow
. By induction on
\Omega_{l}\leq\Omega_{r}
.

–

Case
Le-ToSymbolic
. Trivial.

–

Case
Le-MoveValue
. Case disjunction on wether the borrow
is moved or not; we conclude by
Le-MoveValue
.
Similarly to the
Reorg-End-MutBorrow
case, an important point is that the moved
value can’t come from a region abstraction (this is important because
we can’t end borrows which are inside region abstractions: we have to
end the abstraction first).

–

Case
Le-Fresh-MutLoan
. The ended borrows is necessarily
independent from the fresh mutable loan; we conclude by
Le-Fresh-MutLoan
.

–

Case
Le-Fresh-SharedLoan
. We conclude by
Le-Fresh-SharedLoan
.

–

Case
Le-Reborrow-MutBorrow
. Similar to above.

–

Case
Le-Fresh-SharedBorrow
. On the right we might terminate the fresh
shared borrow in which case we conclude by
Le-AnonValue
; otherwise we
conclude with
Le-Fresh-SharedBorrow
.

–

Case
Le-Reborrow-SharedLoan
. We can not end any of the borrows
inside the fresh region abstraction, meaning we can not end the fresh borrow
nor the borrows inside the shared value. We conclude by
Le-Reborrow-SharedLoan
.

–

Case
Le-Abs-End-SharedLoan
. We conclude by
Le-Abs-End-SharedLoan
.

–

Case
Le-Abs-End-DupSharedBorrow
. We can not directly end
a borrow inside a region abstraction; we conclude by
Le-Abs-End-DupSharedBorrow
.

–

Case
Le-Reborrow-SharedBorrow
.
This one is slightly technical.
There exists
{\Omega}[\,{.}\,]
,
\ell_{0}
,
\ell_{1}
,
v
,
\sigma
,
A_{0}
such that:

\displaystyle\bot,\mathsf{loan}^{m},\mathsf{borrow}^{s,r,m}\notin v

\displaystyle\mathsf{loan}^{s}\,\ell_{0}\,v\in\Omega_{l}

\displaystyle\Omega_{l}={\Omega}[\,{\mathsf{borrow}^{s}\,\ell_{0}}\,]

\displaystyle\Omega_{r}={\Omega}[\,{\mathsf{borrow}^{s}\,\ell_{1}}\,],\,A_{0}\,\{\,\mathsf{borrow}^{s}\,\ell_{0},\,\mathsf{loan}^{s}\,\ell_{1}\,\sigma\,\}

The difficult case happens when we end the borrow
\ell_{1}
, so this is the
case we focus on (in the other case, we conclude by
Le-Reborrow-SharedBorrow
).
We do no reorganization on the left. We have to prove:

\displaystyle{\Omega}[\,{\mathsf{borrow}^{s}\,\ell_{0}}\,]\leq{\Omega}[\,{\bot}\,],\,A_{0}\,\{\,\mathsf{borrow}^{s}\,\ell_{0},\,\mathsf{loan}^{s}\,\ell_{1}\,\sigma\,\}

Because we could end
\ell_{1}
on the right, necessarily the hole on the left is
not inside a shared loan. This means we can move it to an anonymous value
by using
Le-MoveValue
.
We get:

\displaystyle{\Omega}[\,{\ell_{0}}\,]\leq{\Omega}[\,{\bot}\,],\,\_\rightarrow\mathsf{borrow}^{s}\,\ell_{0}

We now apply
Le-Reborrow-SharedBorrow
to
\mathsf{borrow}^{s}\,\ell_{0}
.
We get:

\displaystyle{\Omega}[\,{\ell_{0}}\,]\leq{\Omega}[\,{\bot}\,],\,\_\rightarrow\mathsf{borrow}^{s}\,\ell_{1},\,A_{1}\,\{\,\mathsf{borrow}^{s}\,\ell_{0},\,\mathsf{loan}^{s}\,\ell_{1}\,\sigma\,\}

We apply
Le-ToAbs
to
\mathsf{borrow}^{s}\,\ell_{0}
, then merge the two region
abstractions with
Le-MergeAbs
, taking care of using
MergeAbs-Shared

to get rid of
\mathsf{borrow}^{s}\,\ell_{1}
. QED.

–

Case
Le-ToAbs
.
We can not end a borrow which was moved to a region abstraction; the borrow
we end on the right state was thus not in the anonymous value we converted to
an abstraction; we can end it in the left state and conclude by
Le-ToAbs
.

–

Case
Le-MergeAbs
. Similar to
Le-ToAbs
.

–

Cases
Le-ClearAbs
,
Le-Abs-ClearValue
,

Le-Abs-DeconstructPair
,
Le-Abs-DeconstructSum
. Trivial.

–

Case
Le-AnonValue
. Trivial.

•

Case
Reorg-End-SharedLoan
. By induction on
\Omega_{l}\leq\Omega_{r}
.

–

Case
Le-ToSymbolic
. Trivial.

–

Case
Le-MoveValue
. Trivial.

–

Case
Le-Fresh-MutLoan
. Trivial.

–

Case
Le-Fresh-SharedLoan
. We might end the fresh shared loan,
in which case the right state become equal to the left state; we conclude
by reflexivity of
\leq
.

–

Case
Le-Reborrow-MutBorrow
. Trivial.

–

Case
Le-Fresh-SharedBorrow
. Trivial (we can not end the shared loan
from which we created a new shared borrow).

–

Case
Le-Reborrow-SharedLoan
.
Because there is
\mathsf{borrow}^{s}\,\ell_{1}
in the region abstraction, we can not
end
\mathsf{loan}^{s}\,\ell_{1}\,\sigma
on the right. We can end the shared loan
\ell_{0}
,
if there are no shared borrows for
\ell_{0}
. If it is the case, we do no
reorganization on the left, then use
Le-Reborrow-SharedLoan
in combination
with
Le-Abs-End-SharedLoan
. In the other situations, we can conclude
by
Le-Reborrow-SharedLoan
.

–

Case
Le-Abs-End-SharedLoan
. Trivial.

–

Case
Le-Abs-End-DupSharedBorrow
. Trivial.

–

Case
Le-Reborrow-SharedBorrow
. We can not end any of the shared loans
for
\ell_{0}
or
\ell_{1}
on the right; we easily conclude by
Le-Reborrow-SharedBorrow
.

–

Case
Le-ToAbs
. If we end one of the shared loans which were moved
to the fresh region abstraction, we use
Le-ToAbs
then

Le-Abs-End-SharedLoan
.
Otherwise we end the corresponding shared loan on the left then conclude
by
Le-ToAbs
.

–

Case
Le-MergeAbs
. If the shared loan we end doesn’t appear inside
the merged region abstraction, we end the corresponding loan on the left and conclude
by
Le-MergeAbs
.
Otherwise, we simply use
Le-MergeAbs
then
Le-Abs-EndSharedLoan
.

–

Cases
Le-ClearAbs
,
Le-Abs-ClearValue
,

Le-Abs-DeconstructPair
,
Le-Abs-DeconstructSum
. Trivial.

–

Case
Le-AnonValue
. Trivial.

•

Case
Reorg-Activate-Reserved
. By induction on
\Omega_{l}\leq\Omega_{r}
.

–

Case
Le-ToSymbolic
. Trivial.

–

Case
Le-MoveValue
. Trivial.

–

Case
Le-Fresh-MutLoan
. Trivial.

–

Case
Le-Fresh-SharedLoan
. Trivial.

–

Case
Le-Reborrow-MutBorrow
. Trivial.

–

Case
Le-Fresh-SharedBorrow
. The borrow we activate can’t have
the same identifier as the fresh shared borrow; we can thus activate the
same borrow on the left and conclude by
Le-Fresh-SharedBorrow
.

–

Case
Le-Reborrow-SharedLoan
. The borrow we activate can not be
\ell_{1}
because
there is a corresponding shared borrow, nor
\ell_{0}
, because the shared
loan is in a region abstraction. We can thus activate the same borrow on
the left and conclude by
Le-Reborrow-SharedLoan
.

–

Case
Le-Abs-End-SharedLoan
. Trivial.

–

Case
Le-Abs-End-DupSharedBorrow
. Trivial.

–

Case
Le-Reborrow-SharedBorrow
. Similar to
Le-Reborrow-SharedLoan
.

–

Case
Le-ToAbs
. We can not move reserved borrows to region abstractions,
and can not activate borrows associated to loans in region abstractions.
We can thus activate the same borrow on the left and conclude by
Le-ToAbs
.

–

Case
Le-MergeAbs
. Similar to
Le-ToAbs
.

–

Cases
Le-ClearAbs
,
Le-Abs-ClearValue
,

Le-Abs-DeconstructPair
,
Le-Abs-DeconstructSum
. Trivial.

–

Case
Le-AnonValue
. Trivial.

•

Case
Reorg-End-Abstraction
. By induction on
\Omega_{l}\leq\Omega_{r}
.

–

Case
Le-ToSymbolic
. Trivial.

–

Case
Le-MoveValue
. Trivial (the moved value can’t be inside a region
abstraction; we can thus end the
region abstraction on the left and conclude by
Le-MoveValue
).

–

Case
Le-Fresh-MutLoan
. Trivial (we can’t end a region abstraction
in which there is a loan).

–

Case
Le-Fresh-SharedLoan
. Trivial (similar to the case

Le-Fresh-MutLoan
).

–

Case
Le-Reborrow-MutBorrow
. The mutable borrow might be inside
the region abstraction we end, but we can still conclude
Le-Reborrow-MutBorrow
.
We also conclude by
Le-Reborrow-MutBorrow
in the other cases.

–

Case
Le-Fresh-SharedBorrow
. Trivial (note that we can’t end a region
abstraction in which there is a loan).

–

Case
Le-Reborrow-SharedLoan
. Trivial (note that we can’t end a region
abstraction in which there is a loan).

–

Case
Le-Abs-End-SharedLoan
. We might end the region abstraction
in which we just ended the loan. In this case, we also end the loan and
the region abstraction on the left, and conclude by reflexivity of
\leq
.
Otherwise, we end the corresponding region on the left, and conclude
by
Le-Abs-End-SharedLoan
.

–

Case
Le-Abs-End-DupSharedBorrow
.
If we end the region abstraction from which we removed the duplicated borrow,
we can end the same region abstraction on the left, and end one of the
borrows which were reintroduced into the context.
We then need to remove the anonymous variable containing
\bot
that is
left in place of the borrow (there is no primitive rule for
\leq
to do
that, but we can actually achieve the same result by using
Le-ToAbs

on this value).

–

Case
Le-Reborrow-SharedBorrow
. Trivial.

–

Case
Le-ToAbs
. If the abstraction we end is not the one we just
introduced, we can end the corresponding abstraction on the left an conclude
by
Le-ToAbs
. If it is the same region abstraction, then this abstraction
doesn’t contain any loans. This means that the anonymous value we converted
to a region abstraction only contains borrows (we get this by a simple
induction on the value). Also, it doesn’t contain nested borrows
(because it doesn’t contain shared loans, and because of the premises of

ToAbs-MutBorrow
).
Finally, the mutably borrowed values don’t contain
\bot
(again, because
of the premises of
ToAbs-MutBorrow
).
We can thus apply
Le-ToSymbolic
on all the mutably borrowed value
of the anonymous value
(note that when ending a region abstraction, we reintroduce the mutable
borrows with fresh symbolic values in the context; see
Reorg-End-Abstraction
).
We can then move all the borrows from the anonymous to their own anonymous
values (by
Le-MoveValue
; we showed before that those borrows
don’t appear in shared loans) and finally get rid of the original anonymous
value by using the same technique as in the case
Le-Abs-End-DupSharedBorrow
.

–

Case
Le-MergeAbs
. If the abstraction we end is not the merged abstraction,
we end the corresponding abstraction on the left and conclude by
Le-MergeAbs
.
If it is the same abstraction, we can end the first merged abstraction on the left,
the borrows it reintroduced in the context and whose corresponding loans
are in the second abstraction, then the second abstraction.
In particular, we note that we could end the merged abstraction only if,
for all pairs borrow/loan such that the borrow was in the first abstraction
and the loan in the second, we correctly used
Merge-AbsMut
.
The borrows consumed during the merge by using
Merge-AbsMut
are the ones
we need to end on the left, after ending the first abstraction, so that we
can end the second. Finally, we conclude by the reflexivity of
\leq
.

–

Cases
Le-ClearAbs
,
Le-Abs-ClearValue
,

Le-Abs-DeconstructPair
,
Le-Abs-DeconstructSum
. Trivial.

–

Case
Le-AnonValue
. Trivial.

We now turn to the proof of the target theorem.

Theorem C.4 (Eval-Preserves-LLBC+-Rel).

For all
\Omega_{l}
and
\Omega_{r}

\text{LLBC}^{+}
states we have:

\displaystyle\Omega_{l}\leq\Omega_{r}\Rightarrow\forall\;s\,v_{r}\,\Omega^{\prime}_{r},\ \Omega_{r}\vdash_{\mathsf{llbc+}}s\rightsquigarrow(r,\,\Omega^{\prime}_{r})\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\exists\;\Omega^{\prime}_{l},\ \Omega_{l}\vdash_{\mathsf{llbc+}}s\Downarrow(r,\,\Omega^{\prime}_{l})\wedge\Omega^{\prime}_{l}\leq\Omega^{\prime}_{r}

Proof

By induction on
\Omega_{r}\vdash_{\mathsf{llbc+}}\,s\rightsquigarrow\rightsquigarrow(r,\,\Omega_{r}^{\prime})
.

•

Case empty statement. Trivial.

•

Case
E-Reorg
. By Lemma
C.3
.

•

Case
s_{0};\;s_{1}
. We use the induction hypotheses, then have to consider
all the states resulting from evaluating
s_{0}
; this is straightforward.

•

Case
p:=rv
. By Lemma
C.2
.

•

Case
if␣then␣else
. Trivial by the induction hypotheses.

•

Case
match
. Trivial by the induction hypotheses.

•

Case
\mathsf{free}\,p
. The reasoning is similar to the
\mathsf{move}\,p
and
p:=

cases in Lemmas
C.1
and
C.2
.
The proof is straightforward by induction on
\Omega_{l}\leq\Omega_{r}
.

•

Case
\mathsf{return}
. Trivial.

•

Cases
\mathsf{panic}
,
\mathsf{break}\,i
,
\mathsf{continue}\,i
. Trivial.

•

Case loop. Trivial by the induction hypotheses.

•

Case function call.
Similar to the same case in the proof of
Eval-Preserves-HLPL+-Rel
.
Note that we study
\text{LLBC}^{+}
here, not
\text{LLBC}^{\#}
, meaning the rule we need to consider
is
E-Call
, not
E-Call-Symbolic
.
We use the Lemma
C.1
to relate the states resulting
from evaluating the operands given as inputs to the function (trivial induction
on the number of inputs).
We easily get that the states resulting from
\vdash\mathsf{push\_stack}\;
are
related (again, induction on the number of inputs then on the number of
local variables which are not used as inputs).
The induction hypothesis gives us that the states are related after evaluation
of
body
. If the tag is
\mathsf{panic}
we are done.
If it is
\mathsf{return}
, we get that the states resulting from
\vdash\mathsf{pop\_stack}\;

are related (induction on the number of local variables, and we use

C.2
for the assignments which “drop” the local variables).
Finally, we do a reasoning similar to what we did in

C.2
to show that the states are still related after
assigning to the destination.

\text{LLBC}^{+}
and
\text{LLBC}^{\#}

\mathsf{init}
,
\mathsf{final}
,
\mathsf{inst-sig}

in figures

,

,

and

Init and Final
\text{LLBC}^{\#}
States for Borrow Checking and Signature Instantiation

The following substitution lemmas are trivial but crucial in several situations.

Lemma D.1 (Le-Subst).

For all
\Omega_{l}
,
\Omega_{r}

\text{LLBC}^{+}
states
and
\mathsf{subst}
identifier substitution we have (where
\mathsf{subst}\;\Omega

is a pointwise substitution):

\displaystyle\Omega_{l}\leq\Omega_{r}\Rightarrow\mathsf{subst}\;\Omega_{l}\leq\mathsf{subst}\;\Omega_{r}

Proof.
The proof is straightforward by induction on

\Omega\leq\Omega^{\prime}
.

•

Reflexive case. Trivial.

•

Transitive case. Trivial by the induction hypothesis.

•

Case
Le-ToSymbolic
.
There exists
\Omega[.]
,
v
and
\sigma
fresh such that:

\text{borrows, loans, $\bot$}\not\in v\;\wedge\;\Omega_{l}=\Omega[v]\;\wedge\;\Omega_{r}=\Omega[\sigma]

A trivial induction on
v
gives us that, as there are no borrows, loans
and
\bot
in
v
(premise of the rule) then there are no borrows, loans
and
\bot
in
\mathsf{subst}\;v
.
Moreover, as
\mathsf{subst}
is injective we have that
\mathsf{subst}\;\sigma

if fresh for
\Omega_{l}
. We pose:

\displaystyle\Omega^{\prime}[.]:=(\mathsf{subst}\;\Omega)[.]

\displaystyle v^{\prime}:=\mathsf{subst}\;v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\sigma^{\prime}:=\mathsf{subst}\;\sigma

We have (trivial induction on
\Omega[.]
, comes from the fact that

\mathsf{subst}
is applied pointwise):

\displaystyle\mathsf{subst}\;\Omega_{l}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{subst}\;(\Omega[v])

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{subst}\;\Omega)[\mathsf{subst}\;v])

\displaystyle=\Omega^{\prime}[v^{\prime}]

Similarly, we have:
\mathsf{subst}\;\Omega_{r}=\Omega^{\prime}[\sigma^{\prime}]
.
Hence:
\mathsf{subst}\;\Omega_{l}\leq\mathsf{subst}\;\Omega_{r}
.

•

Case
Le-MoveValue
. Trivial. We use the fact that the substitution
is pointwise (like in the
Le-ToSymbolic
case).

•

Case
Le-Fresh-MutLoan
. Similar to the
Le-ToSymbolic
case.

•

Case
Le-Fresh-SharedLoan
. Similar to the
Le-ToSymbolic
case.

•

Case
Le-Reborrow-MutBorrow
. Similar to the
Le-ToSymbolic
case.

•

Case
Le-Fresh-SharedBorrow
. Similar to the
Le-ToSymbolic
case.

•

Case
Le-Reborrow-SharedLoan
. Similar to the
Le-ToSymbolic
case.

•

Case
Le-Abs-End-SharedLoan
. Similar to the
Le-ToSymbolic
case.

•

Case
Le-Abs-End-DupSharedBorrow
.Similar to the
Le-ToSymbolic
case.

•

Case
Le-Reborrow-SharedBorrow
. Similar to the
Le-ToSymbolic
case.

•

Case
Le-ToAbs
.
We need the auxiliary lemma:

\forall v\vec{A},\;v\prec^{\text{to-abs}}\vec{A}\Rightarrow\mathsf{subst}\;v\prec^{\text{to-abs}}\mathsf{subst}\;(\prec^{\text{to-abs}}\vec{A})

The proof is straightforward by induction on
v\prec^{\text{to-abs}}\vec{A}
.

•

Case
Le-MergeAbs
.
We need the auxiliary lemma:

\forall A_{0}\;A_{1}\;A,\;\vdash A_{0}\Join A_{1}=A\Rightarrow\vdash\mathsf{subst}\;A_{0}\Join\mathsf{subst}\;A_{1}=\mathsf{subst}\;A

The proof is straightforward by induction on
\vdash A_{0}\Join A_{1}=A
.

•

Cases
Le-ClearAbs
,
Le-Abs-ClearValue
,

Le-Abs-DeconstructPair
,
Le-Abs-DeconstructSum
,
Le-AnonValue
.
Similar to the
Le-MoveValue
case.

Lemma D.2 (Eval-Subst-
\text{LLBC}^{+}
).

For all
\Omega
,
\Omega^{\prime}

\text{LLBC}^{+}
states,
s
statement,
r
control-flow
tag and
\mathsf{subst}
identifier substitution we have:

\displaystyle\Omega\leq\Omega^{\prime}\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\Omega\vdash s\rightsquigarrow(r,\,\Omega^{\prime})\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\mathsf{subst}\;\Omega\vdash s\rightsquigarrow(r,\,\mathsf{subst}\;\Omega^{\prime})

Proof.

Like with the proof of the simulation for
\text{LLBC}^{+}
, we have to introduce auxiliary lemmas
for evaluating rvalues and applying reorganizations.
All the proofs are straightforward by induction on the evaluation or reorganization
derivations, and are actually very similar to the proof of Lemma
D.1
.

We have a lemma similar to the above one for
\text{LLBC}^{\#}
evaluations (omitted).

We need the following framing lemma for
\leq
. Note that as the transformation rules for

\leq
are quite local we don’t need very strong disjointness conditions.
The disjointness condition is given by
\mathsf{le\_framable}
and states
that: 1. the fresh identifiers introduced in
\Omega^{\prime}
are not present in the
frame (this allows us to preserve freshness);
2. the initial state
\Omega
and the frame
\Omega_{f}
don’t use the same identifiers, put
aside the fact that we allow some borrows in the partial state
\Omega
to be dangling, by
pointing to loans in the frame
\Omega_{f}
(we will need this condition when handling function calls).

Lemma D.3 (Frame Rule for
\leq
in
\text{LLBC}^{+}
).

For all
\Omega
,
\Omega^{\prime}

\text{LLBC}^{+}
states, we have:

\displaystyle\Omega\leq\Omega^{\prime}\Rightarrow\forall\;\Omega_{f},\;\mathsf{framable}\;{\Omega}\;{\Omega^{\prime}}\;{\Omega_{f}}\Rightarrow\quad\Omega\cup\Omega_{f}\leq\Omega^{\prime}\cup\Omega_{f}

where:

\displaystyle\mathsf{framable}\;{\Omega}\;{\Omega^{\prime}}\;{\Omega_{f}}:=

(1)

\displaystyle\quad(\forall\;\ell\in\Omega^{\prime},\;\ell\notin\Omega\Rightarrow\ell\notin\Omega_{f})\;\wedge\;

(2)

\displaystyle\quad(\forall\;\sigma,\;\sigma\in\Omega\;\vee\;\sigma\in\Omega^{\prime}\Rightarrow\sigma\notin\Omega_{f})\;\wedge\;

(3)

\displaystyle\quad(\forall\;A,\;A\in\Omega\;\vee\;A\in\Omega^{\prime}\Rightarrow A\notin\Omega_{f})\;\wedge\;

\displaystyle\quad(\forall\;\ell\in\Omega,\;\ell\in\Omega_{f}\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\qquad(\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell\;v\in\Omega\;\wedge\;\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell\notin\Omega)\;\vee\;

(4)

\displaystyle\qquad(\mathsf{borrow}^{s}\,\ell\in\Omega\;\wedge\;\mathsf{borrow}^{s}\,\ell\in\Omega^{\prime}\;\wedge\;\mathsf{loan}^{s}\,\ell\,v\notin\Omega^{\prime}))

Proof.

We do the proof by induction on
\Omega\leq\Omega^{\prime}
.

•

Reflexive case. Trivial.

•

Transitive case.
This is the difficult case: if we want to use the induction hypotheses we have
to pay attention to the
\mathsf{framable}
condition. We take care of this
mostly by using the substitution lemma
D.1
.

More precisely, the facts
\mathsf{framable}\;{\Omega}\;{\Omega^{\prime\prime}}\;{\Omega_{f}}

and
\mathsf{framable}\;{\Omega^{\prime\prime}}\;{\Omega^{\prime}}\;{\Omega_{f}}
don’t necessarily hold.
However, we can build
\mathsf{subst}
s.t.:

\displaystyle\mathsf{framable}\;{\Omega}\;{\mathsf{subst}\;\Omega^{\prime\prime}}\;{\Omega_{f}}\;\wedge\;

\displaystyle\mathsf{framable}\;{\mathsf{subst}\;\Omega^{\prime\prime}}\;{\Omega^{\prime}}\;{\Omega_{f}}\;\wedge\;

\displaystyle\Omega\leq\mathsf{subst}\;\Omega^{\prime\prime}\leq\Omega^{\prime}

For instance, if there exists
\sigma\in\Omega^{\prime\prime}
, we have to prove
that
\sigma\notin\Omega_{f}
. If
\sigma\in\Omega
or
\sigma\notin\Omega^{\prime}

we simply use (2). However, it may happen that
\sigma\notin\Omega
and

\sigma\notin\Omega^{\prime}
, if a transformation (e.g.,
Le-ToSymbolic
) introduces a fresh
\sigma

when transforming
\Omega
to
\Omega^{\prime\prime}
, then another transformation (e.g.,

Le-MoveValue
then
Le-ToAbs
) removes it when transforming

\Omega^{\prime\prime}
to
\Omega^{\prime}
. In this situation, we just have to pick

\sigma^{\prime}
such that
\sigma^{\prime}\notin\Omega,\;\Omega^{\prime}\;\Omega^{\prime\prime},\;\Omega_{f}

and pose
\mathsf{subst}
such that
\mathsf{subst}
is the identity but on

\sigma
where we define it as:
\mathsf{subst}\;\sigma=\sigma^{\prime}
. We then
use
D.1
to prove:
\Omega\leq\mathsf{subst}\;\Omega^{\prime\prime}\leq\Omega^{\prime}
.
By using this technique, we can make sure (2) and (3) are satisfied for

\Omega
and
\Omega^{\prime\prime}
, and for
\Omega^{\prime\prime}
and
\Omega^{\prime}
; that is:

\displaystyle(\forall\;\sigma,\;\sigma\in\Omega\;\vee\;\sigma\in\Omega^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow\sigma\notin\Omega_{f})\;\wedge\;

\displaystyle(\forall\;A,\;A\in\Omega\;\vee\;A\in\Omega^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow A\notin\Omega_{f})

and:

\displaystyle(\forall\;\sigma,\;\sigma\in\Omega^{\prime\prime}\;\vee\;\sigma\in\Omega^{\prime}\Rightarrow\sigma\notin\Omega_{f})\;\wedge\;

\displaystyle(\forall\;A,\;A\in\Omega^{\prime\prime}\;\vee\;A\in\Omega^{\prime}\Rightarrow A\notin\Omega_{f})

(1) and (4) are more difficult.
We can make sure that (1) is satisfied for
\Omega
and
\Omega^{\prime\prime}
(same technique).
Then, given
\ell\in\Omega^{\prime}
such that
\ell\notin\Omega^{\prime\prime}
, we want to
show that
\ell\notin\Omega_{f}
. If
\ell\notin\Omega
it is trivial by (1).
However, it may happen that
\ell\in\Omega
: it means that
\ell
was originally
in
\Omega
, that we removed it (because of
Le-Abs-End-SharedLoan
or
MergeAbs-Mut
),
then reused it in a rule which introduces a fresh loan identifier.
But this is actually forbidden by (4), and we use this fact.

By induction on
\Omega\leq\Omega^{\prime}
we prove that:

\displaystyle(\forall\;\ell\in\Omega^{\prime\prime},\;\ell\in\Omega_{f}\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\quad(\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell\;v\in\Omega^{\prime\prime}\;\wedge\;\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell\notin\Omega^{\prime\prime})\;\vee\;

\displaystyle\quad(\mathsf{borrow}^{s}\,\ell\in\Omega^{\prime\prime}\;\wedge\;\mathsf{borrow}^{s}\,\ell\in\Omega^{\prime}\;\wedge\;\mathsf{loan}^{s}\,\ell\,v\notin\Omega^{\prime}))

We then reuse this to prove (by contradiction and) by induction on
\Omega^{\prime\prime}\leq\Omega^{\prime}
that:

\forall\;\ell\in\Omega^{\prime},\;\ell\notin\Omega^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow\ell\notin\Omega_{f}
.

This allows us to use the induction hypotheses, and conclude the proof.

•

Case
Le-ToSymbolic
.
There exists
\Omega_{h}[.]
,
v
and
\sigma
fresh for
\Omega_{l}
such that:

\text{borrows, loans, $\bot$}\not\in v\;\wedge\;\Omega=\Omega_{h}[v]\;\wedge\;\Omega^{\prime}=\Omega_{h}[\sigma]

Because of (2) in the
\mathsf{framable}\;{\Omega}\;{\Omega^{\prime}}\;{\Omega_{f}}
assumption we have
that
\sigma
is fresh for
\Omega\cup\Omega_{f}
.
Posing
\Omega^{\prime}_{h}[.]:=\Omega_{h}[.]\cup\Omega_{f}
we get:

\Omega\cup\Omega_{f}=\Omega^{\prime}_{h}[v]\\
\Omega^{\prime}\cup\Omega_{f}=\Omega^{\prime}_{h}[\sigma]

Hence:
\Omega\cup\Omega_{f}\leq\Omega^{\prime}\cup\Omega_{f}
.

•

Case
Le-MoveValue
. Trivial (we don’t introduce fresh identifiers).

•

Case
Le-Fresh-MutLoan
. Similar to the
Le-ToSymbolic
case.

•

Case
Le-Fresh-SharedLoan
. Similar to the
Le-ToSymbolic
case.

•

Case
Le-Reborrow-MutBorrow
. Similar to the
Le-ToSymbolic
case.

•

Case
Le-Fresh-SharedBorrow
. Similar to the
Le-ToSymbolic
case.

•

Case
Le-Reborrow-SharedLoan
. Similar to the
Le-ToSymbolic
case.

•

Case
Le-Abs-End-SharedLoan
. We use (4) to prove that there is no

\mathsf{borrow}^{s,r}\,\ell\in\Omega_{f}
.

•

Case
Le-Abs-End-DupSharedBorrow
. Similar to the
Le-ToSymbolic
case.

•

Case
Le-Reborrow-SharedBorrow
. Similar to the
Le-ToSymbolic
case.

•

Case
Le-ToAbs
. Similar to the
Le-ToSymbolic
case.

•

Case
Le-MergeAbs
. Trivial.

•

Cases
Le-ClearAbs
,
Le-Abs-ClearValue
,

Le-Abs-DeconstructPair
,
Le-Abs-DeconstructSum
,
Le-AnonValue
. Trivial.

We need the framing lemma below for evaluation.
We have the same disjointness conditions as for the
\leq
framing lemma
(
D.3
).

Lemma D.4 (Frame Rule for Evaluation in
\text{LLBC}^{+}
).

For all
\Omega
,
\Omega^{\prime}

\text{LLBC}^{+}
states, we have:

\displaystyle\Omega\vdash s\rightsquigarrow(r,\,\Omega^{\prime})\Rightarrow\forall\;\Omega_{f},\;\mathsf{framable}\;{\Omega}\;{\Omega^{\prime}}\;{\Omega_{f}}\Rightarrow\quad\Omega\cup\Omega_{f}\vdash s\rightsquigarrow(r,\,\Omega^{\prime}\cup\Omega_{f})

Proof.
The proof is similar to the proof of
D.3
.
Like with the simulation proofs, we have to split it into several auxiliarly
lemmas for the evaluation of rvalues and for reorganizations.
Exactly like with
D.3
, the difficult cases are:
1. the transitive case of reorganization, because it allows us to remove
borrows from the context; 2. the cases for statement evaluationwhere
we need to use the induction hypotheses (typically,
E-Seq-Unit

and
E-Reorg
). We handle those by using the same strategy as in
D.3
.

We now move on to the proof of Theorem
4.1
. We do the proof by
induction on the step index. In order to do so we have to generalize the theorem
statement a bit.

Lemma D.5.

For all
\Omega
and
\Omega^{\#}

\text{LLBC}^{\#}
states, step
n
, statement
s
, and
S^{\#}
set of
states with outcomes, we have:

\displaystyle(\forall\;f\in\mathcal{P},\;\mathsf{borrow\_checks}\;{f})\Rightarrow\Omega\leq\Omega^{\#}\Rightarrow\Omega^{\#}\vdash_{\mathsf{llbc\#}}\,s\rightsquigarrow S^{\#}\Rightarrow

\displaystyle(\Omega\vdash_{\mathsf{llbc}}s\underset{n}{\rightsquigarrow}\infty)\;\vee\;(\exists\;\Omega_{1},\,\Omega\vdash_{\mathsf{llbc}}s\underset{n}{\rightsquigarrow}(\mathsf{panic},\,\Omega_{1}))\;\vee\;

\displaystyle(\exists\;r\,\Omega_{1}\,\Omega^{\#}_{1},\,r\in\{(),\,\mathsf{return},\,\mathsf{break}\,i,\,\mathsf{continue}\,i\}\;\wedge\;

\displaystyle\quad\Omega\vdash_{\mathsf{llbc}}\,s\rightsquigarrow(r,\,\Omega_{1})\;\wedge\;\Omega_{1}\leq\Omega^{\#}_{1}\;\wedge\;(r,\;\Omega^{\#}_{1})\in S^{\#})

Proof.
We do the proof by induction on the step index.

Case
n=0
: trivial.

Case
n=n^{\prime}+1
:

We do it by induction on the statement
s
. Note that because we do not dive into
function calls, we can prove the statement by induction on the syntax rather
than on the derivation rules.

When the statement does not contain a function call, we can reuse the proofs
that there is a forward simulation from
\text{LLBC}^{\#}
to
\text{LLBC}^{\#}
(also note that only function
calls decrease the step index).

There remains the interesting case, that is a call to some function

\mathsf{fn}\ \langle{\overrightarrow{\rho}}\rangle\ (\overrightarrow{x_{i}}:\overrightarrow{\tau_{i}})\ (\overrightarrow{y_{j}}:\overrightarrow{\tau_{j}})\ (x_{\mathsf{ret}}:\tau)\ \{\ s\ \}
).
We use the fact that the rule
E-Call-Symbolic
and the predicate
\mathsf{borrow\_checks}
were
written so that they "match".

Let us illustrate how we do on an example.
We start in the state
\Omega_{0}
below and evaluate
let␣z␣=␣choose(true,␣move␣px,␣move␣py)
:

\displaystyle\smaller\Omega_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x0\mapsto\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell_{\ell_{0}}

\displaystyle y0\mapsto\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell_{\ell_{1}}

\displaystyle px\mapsto\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell_{\ell_{0}}\;0

\displaystyle py\mapsto\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell_{\ell_{1}}\;1

After pushing the stack (
E-PushStack
) we get
\Omega_{1}
below (the field

\mathsf{stack}
of the state, which contains the list of all the pushed stack variables, is implicit):

\displaystyle\smaller\Omega_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x0\mapsto\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell_{0}

\displaystyle y0\mapsto\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell_{1}

\displaystyle px\mapsto\bot

\displaystyle py\mapsto\bot

\displaystyle x_{\mathsf{ret}}\mapsto\bot

\displaystyle x\mapsto\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell_{0}\;0

\displaystyle y\mapsto\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell_{1}\;1

The borrow checking assumption gives us that there
exists
some loan identifiers,
symbolic values, states
\Omega_{\mathsf{init}}
and
\Omega_{\mathsf{final}}
, and a set of
states with outcomes
S^{\#}
such that:

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathsf{init}}\vdash\mathsf{choose}.\mathsf{body}\rightsquigarrow S^{\#}\;\wedge\;

\displaystyle\forall\;res\in S^{\#},\;\exists\;\Omega^{\#},\;res=(\mathsf{panic},\;\Omega^{\#})\;\vee\;(res=(\mathsf{return},\;\Omega^{\#})\;\wedge\;\Omega^{\#}\leq\Omega^{\#}_{\mathsf{final}})

where:

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathsf{init}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{A}\,\{\,\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell^{0}_{x}\;\_,\;\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell^{0}_{y}\;\_,\;\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell_{x},\;\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell_{y}\,\},

\displaystyle x_{\mathsf{ret}}\mapsto\bot

\displaystyle x\mapsto\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell_{x}\;\sigma_{x},\;

\displaystyle y\mapsto\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell_{y}\;\sigma_{y}),\;

and:

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathsf{final}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{A}\,\{\,\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell^{0}_{x}\;\_,\;\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell^{0}_{y}\;\_,\;\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell_{z}\,\},

\displaystyle x_{\mathsf{ret}}\mapsto\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell_{z}\;\sigma_{z}

\displaystyle x\mapsto\bot,\;

\displaystyle y\mapsto\bot,\;

We want to transform
\Omega_{1}
(by using
\leq
rules) in order to isolate
a local state which is equal to
\Omega_{\mathsf{init}}
, then apply the framing
rules.
One issue is that the borrow checking predicate fixes a choice
of loans, symbolic values and region abstractions identifiers (the
\ell_{x}
,
\ell_{y}
,
etc. above), which is not
necessarily the choice which suits us (the borrow checking predicate gives us
a “there exists”, while we want a “for all”).
Fortunately, we can apply the substitution lemmas to get the identifiers we want
(
D.1
and
D.2
).

Another issue is that the “dangling” borrows in environment
\Omega_{\mathsf{init}}

are pairwise disjoint. This is not necessarily the case in environment
\Omega_{0}

for two reasons: 1. we don’t enforce a well-formedness condition on the
state in the assumptions of our theorem, meaning there can be duplicated mutable
borrows (though in practice it won’t happen of course); 2. it is perfectly valid
to use several times the same shared borrows.
We can take care of this problem by introducing reborrowing abstractions
(
A_{0}
and
A_{1}
below, and
\overrightarrow{A_{\mathsf{reborrow}}(\rho)}
in the proof of the general case
afterwards)
with rules
Le-Reborrow-MutBorrow
and
Le-Reborrow-SharedBorrow-Abs
: as those rules introduce fresh loan identifiers,
we make sure the external “dangling” borrows are pairwise distinct.

Coming back to the example with
choose
, by repeatedly applying

Le-Reborrow-MutBorrow-Abs
,
Le-Reborrow-SharedBorrow
,
Le-MergeAbs
and

Le-ToSymbolic
, we get that
\Omega_{1}\leq\Omega_{2}
, where:

\displaystyle\smaller\Omega_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x0\mapsto\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell_{0},

\displaystyle y0\mapsto\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell_{1},

\displaystyle px\mapsto\bot,

\displaystyle py\mapsto\bot,

\displaystyle A_{0}\,\{\,\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell_{0}\;\_,\;\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell^{0}_{x}\,\},

\displaystyle A_{1}\,\{\,\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell_{1}\;\_,\;\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell^{0}_{y}\,\},

// Frame is above, local state is below

\displaystyle{A}\,\{\,\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell^{0}_{x}\;\_,\;\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell^{0}_{y}\;\_,\;\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell_{x},\;\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell_{y}\,\},

\displaystyle x\mapsto\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell_{x}\;\sigma_{x},

\displaystyle y\mapsto\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell_{y}\;\sigma_{y},

\displaystyle x_{\mathsf{ret}}\mapsto\bot

We now apply the frame rule and get that:

\displaystyle\Omega_{2}\vdash\mathsf{choose}.\mathsf{body}\rightsquigarrow S^{\#}\;\wedge\;

\displaystyle\forall\;res\in S^{\#},\;\exists\;\Omega^{\#},\;res=(\mathsf{panic},\;\Omega^{\#})\;\vee\;(res=(\mathsf{return},\;\Omega^{\#})\;\wedge\;\Omega^{\#}\leq\Omega^{\#}_{3})

where:

\displaystyle\smaller\Omega_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x0\mapsto\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell_{0},

\displaystyle y0\mapsto\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell_{1},

\displaystyle px\mapsto\bot,

\displaystyle py\mapsto\bot,

\displaystyle A_{0}\,\{\,\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell_{0}\;\_,\;\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell^{0}_{x}\,\},

\displaystyle A_{1}\,\{\,\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell_{1}\;\_,\;\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell^{0}_{y}\,\},

// Frame is above, local state is below

\displaystyle A_{\mathsf{final}}\,\{\,\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell^{0}_{x}\;\_,\;\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell^{0}_{y}\;\_,\;\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell_{z}\,\},

\displaystyle x\mapsto\bot,\;

\displaystyle y\mapsto\bot,\;

\displaystyle x_{\mathsf{ret}}\mapsto\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell_{z}\;\sigma_{z}

We pop the stack of
\Omega_{3}
(rule
E-PopStack
) and apply the assignemnt to
z
, and get
\Omega_{4}

(once again, we make the
\mathsf{stack}
field explicit):

\displaystyle\smaller\Omega_{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x0\mapsto\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell_{0},

\displaystyle y0\mapsto\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell_{1},

\displaystyle px\mapsto\bot,

\displaystyle py\mapsto\bot,

\displaystyle A_{0}\,\{\,\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell_{0}\;\_,\;\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell^{0}_{x}\,\},

\displaystyle A_{1}\,\{\,\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell_{1}\;\_,\;\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell^{0}_{y}\,\},

\displaystyle A_{\mathsf{final}}\,\{\,\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell^{0}_{x}\;\_,\;\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell^{0}_{y}\;\_,\;\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell_{z}\,\},

\displaystyle z\mapsto\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell_{z}\;\sigma_{z}

We finally merge the reborrowing abstractions (
A_{0}
and
A_{1}
) into
A_{\mathsf{final}}
and
get (this is exactly the state resulting from applying the rule
E-Call-Symbolic
):

\displaystyle\smaller\Omega_{5}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x0\mapsto\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell_{0},

\displaystyle y0\mapsto\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell_{1},

\displaystyle px\mapsto\bot,

\displaystyle py\mapsto\bot,

\displaystyle A_{\mathsf{final}}\,\{\,\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell_{0}\;\_,\;\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell_{1}\;\_,\;\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell_{z}\,\},

\displaystyle z\mapsto\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell_{z}\;\sigma_{z}

We illustrated the proof on the example of
choose
; we now come back to the general
case.

We need some auxiliary lemmas.

Lemma D.6 (Auxiliary Lemma: Frame Body - Value).

\displaystyle\mathsf{init\_extern}\;{\vec{\rho}}\;{\tau}={\overrightarrow{A^{\mathsf{ext}}(\rho)}}\Rightarrow\mathsf{proj\_output}\;{\vec{\rho}}\;{\tau}=({v^{\prime}},\;\overrightarrow{A^{\mathsf{ext\_in}}(\rho)})\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\left(\forall\;\rho,\;A_{\mathsf{init}}(\rho)=\cup\left(A^{\mathsf{ext}}(\rho)\cup A^{\mathsf{ext\_in}}(\rho)\right)\right)\Rightarrow\mathsf{proj\_output}\;{\vec{\rho}}\;{\tau^{\mathsf{out}}}=({v_{\mathsf{out}}},\;\overrightarrow{A^{\mathsf{out}}(\rho)})\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\left(\forall\;\rho,\;A_{\mathsf{final}}(\rho)=A^{\mathsf{out}}(\rho)\cup A^{\mathsf{ext}}(\rho)\right)\Rightarrow\left(\forall\;\rho,\;A^{\mathsf{in}}(\rho)=\{\;\mathsf{proj\_input}\;\rho\;v\;\}\right)\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\left(\forall\,\mathsf{borrow}^{s}\,\ell\in v,\;\exists\;v,\;\mathsf{loan}^{s}\,\ell\,v\in\Omega^{\#}_{0}\right)\Rightarrow\left(\forall\;\rho,\;A_{\mathsf{sig}}(\rho)=A^{\mathsf{in}}(\rho)\cup A^{\mathsf{out}}(\rho)\right)\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\exists\;\overrightarrow{A_{\mathsf{reborrow}}(\rho)},

\displaystyle\mathsf{let}\ \Omega^{\#}_{\mathsf{beg}}=\Omega^{\#}_{0},\;x\rightarrow V[v:\tau],\;x_{\mathsf{ret}}\rightarrow\bot

\displaystyle\mathsf{let}\ \Omega^{\#}_{f}=\Omega^{\#}_{0},\;\overrightarrow{A_{\mathsf{reborrow}}(\rho)}

\displaystyle\mathsf{let}\ \Omega^{\#}_{\mathsf{init\_local}}=\overrightarrow{A_{\mathsf{init}}(\rho)},\;x\rightarrow V[v^{\prime}:\tau],\;x_{\mathsf{ret}}\rightarrow\bot

\displaystyle\mathsf{let}\ \Omega^{\#}_{\mathsf{init}}=\Omega^{\#}_{\mathsf{init\_local}}\cup\Omega^{\#}_{f}

\displaystyle\mathsf{let}\ \Omega^{\#}_{\mathsf{final\_local}}=\overrightarrow{A_{\mathsf{final}}(\rho)},\;x\rightarrow V[\bot],\;\overrightarrow{y\rightarrow\bot},\;x_{\mathsf{ret}}\rightarrow v_{\mathsf{out}}

\displaystyle\mathsf{let}\ \Omega^{\#}_{\mathsf{final}}=\Omega^{\#}_{\mathsf{final\_local}}\cup\Omega^{\#}_{f}

\displaystyle\mathsf{let}\ \Omega^{\#}_{\mathsf{end}}=\Omega^{\#}_{0},\;\overrightarrow{A_{\mathsf{sig}}(\rho)},\;x\rightarrow V[\bot],\;x_{\mathsf{ret}}\rightarrow v_{\mathsf{out}}

\displaystyle\Omega^{\#}_{\mathsf{beg}}\leq\Omega^{\#}_{\mathsf{init}}\;\wedge\;\Omega_{\mathsf{final}}\leq\Omega^{\#}_{\mathsf{end}}\;\wedge\;\mathsf{framable}\;{\Omega^{\#}_{\mathsf{init\_local}}}\;{\Omega^{\#}_{\mathsf{final\_local}}}\;{\Omega^{\#}_{f}}

Proof.
By induction on
\tau
.

•

Pair. By induction hypotheses and
Le-MergeAbs
.

•

\&\;\tau
. We apply
Le-Reborrow-SharedBorrow
twice: once for the

A_{\mathsf{init}}
abstraction and once for the
A_{\mathsf{reborrow}}
abstraction.
We note that only
InitExtern-Shared
,
ProjOutput-Shared
and
ProjInput-Shared
apply
for the projections (we do case disjunctions on
v
,

\mathsf{init\_extern}\;{\vec{\rho}}\;{\tau}={\overrightarrow{A^{\mathsf{ext}}(\rho)}}
,

\mathsf{proj\_output}\;{\vec{\rho}}\;{\tau}=({v^{\prime}},\;\overrightarrow{A^{\mathsf{ext\_in}}(\rho)})

and
\mathsf{proj\_input}\;\rho\;v
). For
\mathsf{framable}
we leverage the freshness of the
identifiers introduced by the rules and the fact that the
A_{\mathsf{init}}(\rho)
and

A_{\mathsf{final}}(\rho)

abstractions don’t have identifiers which are also used elsewhere, at the exception
of dangling borrows pointing to loans in the
A_{\mathsf{reborrow}}
abstractions.

•

\&\mathsf{mut}\;\tau
. Similar to the
\&\;\tau
case, but this time with
Le-Reborrow-MutBorrow-Abs
.

•

Sum. We apply
Le-ToSymbolic
(there can’t be borrows in sums).

•

Literal. We apply
Le-ToSymbolic
.

Lemma D.7 (Auxiliary Lemma: Frame Body).

\displaystyle\left(\forall\;i,\;\mathsf{init\_extern}\;{\vec{\rho}}\;{\tau_{i}}={\overrightarrow{A_{i}^{\mathsf{ext}}(\rho)}}\right)\Rightarrow\left(\forall\;i,\;\mathsf{proj\_output}\;{\vec{\rho}}\;{\tau_{i}}=({v^{\prime}_{i}},\;\overrightarrow{A_{i}^{\mathsf{ext\_in}}(\rho)})\right)\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\left(\forall\;\rho,\;A_{\mathsf{init}}(\rho)=\underset{i}{\cup}\left(A_{i}^{\mathsf{ext}}(\rho)\cup A_{i}^{\mathsf{ext\_in}}(\rho)\right)\right)\Rightarrow\mathsf{proj\_output}\;{\vec{\rho}}\;{\tau^{\mathsf{out}}}=({v_{\mathsf{out}}},\;\overrightarrow{A^{\mathsf{out}}(\rho)})\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\left(\forall\;\rho,\;A_{\mathsf{final}}(\rho)=A^{\mathsf{out}}(\rho)\cup\left(\underset{i}{\cup}A_{i}^{\mathsf{ext}}(\rho)\right)\right)\Rightarrow\left(\forall\;\rho,\;A^{\mathsf{in}}(\rho)=\{\;\mathsf{proj\_input}\;\rho\;v_{i}\;\}\right)\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\left(\forall\,\mathsf{borrow}^{s}\,\ell\in\overrightarrow{v_{i}},\;\exists\;v,\;\mathsf{loan}^{s}\,\ell\,v\in\Omega^{\#}_{0}\right)\Rightarrow\left(\forall\;\rho,\;A_{\mathsf{sig}}(\rho)=A^{\mathsf{in}}(\rho)\cup A^{\mathsf{out}}(\rho)\right)\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\exists\;\overrightarrow{A_{\mathsf{reborrow}}(\rho)},

\displaystyle\mathsf{let}\ \Omega^{\#}_{\mathsf{beg}}=\Omega^{\#}_{0},\;\overrightarrow{x_{i}\rightarrow v_{i}},\;\overrightarrow{y\rightarrow\bot},\;x_{\mathsf{ret}}\rightarrow\bot

\displaystyle\mathsf{let}\ \Omega^{\#}_{f}=\Omega^{\#}_{0},\;\overrightarrow{A_{\mathsf{reborrow}}(\rho)}

\displaystyle\mathsf{let}\ \Omega^{\#}_{\mathsf{init\_local}}=\overrightarrow{A_{\mathsf{init}}(\rho)},\;\overrightarrow{x_{i}\rightarrow v^{\prime}_{i}},\;\overrightarrow{y\rightarrow\bot},\;x_{\mathsf{ret}}\rightarrow\bot

\displaystyle\mathsf{let}\ \Omega^{\#}_{\mathsf{init}}=\Omega^{\#}_{\mathsf{init\_local}}\cup\Omega^{\#}_{f}

\displaystyle\mathsf{let}\ \Omega^{\#}_{\mathsf{final\_local}}=\overrightarrow{A_{\mathsf{final}}(\rho)},\;\overrightarrow{x_{i}\rightarrow\bot},\;\overrightarrow{y\rightarrow\bot},\;x_{\mathsf{ret}}\rightarrow v_{\mathsf{out}}

\displaystyle\mathsf{let}\ \Omega^{\#}_{\mathsf{final}}=\Omega^{\#}_{\mathsf{final\_local}}\cup\Omega^{\#}_{f}

\displaystyle\mathsf{let}\ \Omega^{\#}_{\mathsf{end}}=\Omega^{\#}_{0},\;\overrightarrow{A_{\mathsf{sig}}(\rho)},\;\overrightarrow{x_{i}\rightarrow\bot},\;\overrightarrow{y\rightarrow\bot},\;x_{\mathsf{ret}}\rightarrow v_{\mathsf{out}}

\displaystyle\Omega^{\#}_{\mathsf{beg}}\leq\Omega^{\#}_{\mathsf{init}}\;\wedge\;\Omega_{\mathsf{final}}\leq\Omega^{\#}_{\mathsf{end}}\;\wedge\;\mathsf{framable}\;{\Omega^{\#}_{\mathsf{init\_local}}}\;{\Omega^{\#}_{\mathsf{final\_local}}}\;{\Omega^{\#}_{f}}

Proof.
By induction on
\mathsf{length}\;\overrightarrow{x_{i}}
.
The base case is trivial: we note that
\overrightarrow{A_{\mathsf{init}}(\rho)}=\emptyset
and

\overrightarrow{A_{\mathsf{final}}(\rho)}=\overrightarrow{A_{\mathsf{sig}}(\rho)}
, and pose

\overrightarrow{A_{\mathsf{reborrow}}(\rho)}=\emptyset
.
In the recursive case, the list of local input variables is:
x_{n},\;\overrightarrow{x_{i}}
.
We use the induction hypothesis with
\Omega^{\#}_{0}:=\Omega^{\#}_{0},\;x_{n}\rightarrow v_{n}
,
then apply
D.6
to introduce reborrowing abstractions for
v_{n}
.

Given
D.7
, the rest of the proof of the recursive case of

D.5
is straightforward.

Theorem
D.5
implies the target theorem
4.1
.

Appendix E
Proof of Join and Collapse

We show the full rules for the join and collapse operations in Figure
45
,
Figure
46
, Figure
47
, and Figure
48

Figure 48.
Rules to Collapse States

We prove Theorem
5.1
.

We introduce an auxiliary function
\mathsf{proj\_marked}
to formalize what it means to project
values and states to keep only the values coming from the left state or the
values coming from the right state. The term
\mathsf{proj\_marked}\;{l}\;{v}
(respectively,

\mathsf{proj\_marked}\;{r}\;{v}
) is the value
v
where we discard the values marked as coming from
the right (respectively, left) state. We naturally extend this definition to region
abstractions and states.

\displaystyle\mathsf{proj\_marked}\;{l}\;{v}=v

\displaystyle\mathsf{proj\_marked}\;{l}\;{\hbox to9.99pt{\vbox to9.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.0pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{}{}{{}}{}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.0pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{4.99467pt}{4.54445pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 6.91 6.29)} \pgfsys@hbox{62}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.0pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{}{}{{}}{}

\displaystyle\ldots\text{ (Omitting tuples, etc.)}

\displaystyle\forall\;m\in\{l,\,r\},\,\mathsf{proj\_marked}\;{m}\;{{A}\,\{\,\overrightarrow{v_{i}}\,\}}={A}\,\{\,\overrightarrow{\mathsf{proj\_marked}\;{m}\;{v_{i}}}\,\}

\displaystyle\ldots\text{ (Omitting the rules for states)}

We introduce the auxiliary predicate
\mathsf{sloans\_incl}
for the proofs.
We need it because of the premises of
Join-SharedBorrows

and
Join-SharedBorrows-Precise
(which in turn come from the premises of

Le-Reborrow-SharedBorrow
).
The environments
\Omega_{0}
and
\Omega_{1}
on the left of

\Omega_{0},\,\Omega_{1}\vdash\mathsf{join}\left({}_{,}\;v\right)\;{v_{0}}\;{v_{1}}
are actually needed only so that they can be
mentioned by those rules.

\displaystyle\mathsf{sloans\_incl}\;{\Omega}\;{\Omega^{\prime}}:=\forall\;\ell\,v,\,\mathsf{loan}^{s}\,\ell\,v\in\Omega\Rightarrow\exists\;v^{\prime},\,\mathsf{loan}^{s}\,\ell\,v^{\prime}\in\Omega^{\prime}

The proof requires several lemmas.

Lemma E.1 (Join-Values-Le).

For all
\Omega^{0}_{l}
,
\Omega^{0}_{r}
,
\Omega_{l}[.]
,
\Omega_{r}[.]
,

v_{l}
,
v_{r}
,
v_{j}
,
\overrightarrow{A}
we have:

\displaystyle(\forall\;m\in\{l,\,r\},\,\Omega^{0}_{m}\leq\Omega_{m}[v_{m}]\wedge\mathsf{sloans\_incl}\;{\Omega^{0}_{m}}\;{\Omega_{m}}\wedge\mathsf{wf\_join\_hole}\;{\Omega_{m}[.]}\;{v_{m}})\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\Omega^{0}_{l},\,\Omega^{0}_{r}\vdash\mathsf{join}\left(v_{l},\;v_{r}\right)\rightsquigarrow v_{j}\;|\;\overrightarrow{A}\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\forall\;m\in\{l,\,r\},\,\Omega^{0}_{m}\leq\Omega_{m}[v_{j}],\mathsf{proj\_marked}\;{m}\;{\overrightarrow{A}}\wedge\mathsf{sloans\_incl}\;{\Omega^{0}_{m}}\;{(\Omega_{m}[v_{j}],\mathsf{proj\_marked}\;{m}\;{\overrightarrow{A}})}

where:

\displaystyle\mathsf{wf\_join\_hole}\;{\Omega[.]}\;{v}:=

\displaystyle\text{hole of $\Omega[.]$ inside a shared loan}\Rightarrow\bot\notin v

Proof
:

By induction on

\Omega^{0}_{l},\,\Omega^{0}_{r}\vdash\mathsf{join}\left(v_{l},\;v_{r}\right)\rightsquigarrow v_{j}\;|\;\overrightarrow{A}
.

•

Case
Join-Same
: trivial by reflexivity of
\leq
.

•

Case
Join-Symbolic
: we use
Le-ToSymbolic
.

•

Case
Join-Bottom
: we use
Le-MoveValue
. We need the assumption

\mathsf{wf\_join\_hole}\;{\Omega_{m}[.]}\;{v_{m}}
for the premise that the hole of
\Omega_{l}[.]

or
\Omega_{r}[.]
(depending on whether
v_{l}=\bot
or
v_{r}=\bot
) is not inside a shared loan.

•

Case
Join-MutBorrows
: we the the induction hypothesis for the inner
value (with states
\Omega_{l}[\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell_{0}\;[.]]
and

\Omega_{r}[\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell_{1}\;[.]]
) then
Le-Reborrow-MutBorrow-Abs

to introduce the fresh region abstraction with the reborrow.

•

Case
Join-SharedBorrows
: we use
Le-Reborrow-SharedBorrow
; the premises
stating that there are corresponding shared loans in the context are proven by using the
premise of
Join-SharedBorrows
in combination with the assumption

\mathsf{sloans\_incl}\;{\Omega^{0}_{m}}\;{\Omega_{m}}
.

•

Case
Join-MutLoans
: we use
Le-Reborrow-MutLoan-Abs
to insert
a fresh mutable loan and move the current loan to a fresh region abstraction;
the rules for
\prec^{\text{to-abs}}
(
ToAbs-MutBorrow
then
ToAbs-MutLoan
) allow
us to conclude that the fresh region abstraction has the proper shape.

•

Case
Join-SharedLoans
: we use
Le-Reborrow-SharedLoan
.

•

Cases
Join-MutLoan-Other
,
Join-Other-MutLoan
:
we use
Le-Fresh-MutLoan-Abs
to introduce a fresh loan on the side
which doesn’t have one, then use the induction hypothesis.

•

Cases
Join-SharedLoan-Other
,
Join-Other-SharedLoan
:
we use
Le-Fresh-SharedLoan
to introduce a fresh loan on the side
which does’t have one, then use the induction hypothesis.

•

Cases
Join-Tuple
,
Join-Sum
: trivial by the induction
hypotheses.

•

Case
Join-Same-MutBorrow
: trivial by the induction hypothesis.

•

Case
Join-Same-SharedLoan
: trivial by the induction hypothesis;
we have to use the premise that there are no
\bot
in the inner values
to prove that we can maintain the assumption
\mathsf{sloans\_incl}\;{\Omega^{0}_{m}}\;{\Omega_{m}}
.

•

Case
Join-MutBorrows-Precise
: same as for the case

Join-MutBorrows-Precise
, but we have to introduce
additional region abstractions (with
Le-Reborrow-MutBorrow-Abs
).

•

Case
Join-SharedBorrows-Precise
: same as for the case

Join-SharedBorrows-Precise
, but we have to introduce
additional region abstractions (with
Le-Reborrow-SharedBorrow-Abs
).

Lemma E.2 (Join-States-Le).

For all
\Omega^{0}_{l}
,
\Omega^{0}_{r}
,
\Omega_{acc}
,
\Omega_{l}
,
\Omega_{r}
,

\Omega_{j}
we have:

\displaystyle(\forall\;m\in\{l,\,r\},\,\Omega^{0}_{m}\leq((\mathsf{proj\_marked}\;{m}\;{\Omega_{acc}})\cup\Omega_{m})\wedge

\displaystyle\mathsf{sloans\_incl}\;{\Omega^{0}_{m}}\;{((\mathsf{proj\_marked}\;{m}\;{\Omega_{acc}})\cup\Omega_{m}}))\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\Omega^{0}_{l},\,\Omega^{0}_{r}\vdash\mathsf{join}\left(\Omega_{l},\;\Omega_{r}\right)\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{j}\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\forall\;m\in\{l,\,r\},\,\Omega^{0}_{m}\leq\mathsf{proj\_marked}\;{m}\;{(\Omega_{acc}\cup\Omega_{j})}

Proof
:

By induction on

\Omega^{0}_{l},\,\Omega^{0}_{r}\vdash\mathsf{join}\left(\Omega_{l},\;\Omega_{r}\right)\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{j}
.

Case
Join-SameAbs
: we pose
\Omega^{\prime}_{acc}:=\Omega_{acc},\,A
(we add
the region abstraction
A
to
\Omega_{acc}
) and use
the induction hypothesis.

: similar to case

; we pose

\Omega^{\prime}_{acc}:=\Omega_{acc},\_\rightarrow v
and use the induction hypothesis.

Lemma E.3 (Collapse-Merge-Abs-Le).

For all
\Omega_{l}
,
\Omega_{r}
,
\Omega_{acc}
,
\Omega_{l}
,
\Omega_{r}
,

\Omega_{j}
we have:

\displaystyle\vdash A_{0}\Join A_{1}=A\Rightarrow\forall\;m\in\{l,\,r\},\,\mathsf{proj\_marked}\;{m}\;{A_{0}}\Join\mathsf{proj\_marked}\;{m}\;{A_{1}}=\mathsf{proj\_marked}\;{m}\;{A}

Proof
:

By induction on
\vdash A_{0}\Join A_{1}=A
.

Lemma E.4 (Collapse-Le).

For all
\Omega
,
\Omega^{\prime}
we have:

\displaystyle\vdash\Omega\searrow\Omega^{\prime}\Rightarrow\forall\;m\in\{l,\,r\},\,\mathsf{proj\_marked}\;{m}\;{\Omega}\leq\mathsf{proj\_marked}\;{m}\;{\Omega^{\prime}}

Proof
:

By induction on
\vdash\Omega\searrow\Omega^{\prime}
.

Appendix F
Loops

We describe here a more realistic example which uses a loop.
In this example, we recursively dive into a list so as to get a mutable borrow
to its last element. We could then use this borrow to append another list, for instance.
We define the type list as:
\mathsf{List}\,\tau:=\mu X.\,()+(\tau,\,\mathsf{Box}\,X)
.
We also pose
\mathsf{Nil}:=\mathsf{Left}\,()
and
\mathsf{Cons}\,x\,t:=\mathsf{Right}\,(x,\,\mathsf{Box}\,t)
as
syntactic shortcuts.
We start in an environment where
l
mutably borrows a list (symbolic) value from another
variable
l0
.

⬇

//

l_{0}\mapsto\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell_{0},\;l\mapsto\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell_{0}\;\sigma_{0}

loop

{

match

*
l

{

Nil

=>

{

//

l_{0}\mapsto\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell_{0},\;l\mapsto\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell_{0}\;\mathsf{Nil}

break
;

//

No

need

to

compute

a

join

here

}

Cons

=>

{

//

l_{0}\mapsto\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell_{0},\;l\mapsto\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell_{0}\;(\mathsf{Cons}\,\sigma_{1}\,\sigma_{2})

l

=

&
mut

(*(*
l

as

Cons
).1);

//

l_{0}\mapsto\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell_{0},\;\_\mapsto\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell_{0}\;(\mathsf{Cons}\,\sigma_{1}\,\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell_{1}),\;l\mapsto\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell_{1}\;\sigma_{2}

continue
;

//

We

join

with

the

environment

at

the

entry

of

the

loop

}

}

}

At the continue, we convert the anonymous value to a region abstraction
(with
Le-ToAbs
,
ToAbs-MutBorrow
,
ToAbs-Sum
,
ToAbs-Box
,

ToAbs-MutLoan
).
We join the environment at the entry of the loop with the environment at the

continue
. There is nothing to do for
l_0
because its value is unchanged (we use .

Join-Same
).
For
A_{0}
we use
Join-AbsRight
. Finally, for
l
we use

Join-MutBorrows
. We get:

\displaystyle l_{0}\mapsto\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell_{0},

We collapse the environment by merging
A_{0}
and
A_{1}
.
By
MergeAbs-Mut-MarkedLeft
we simplify
\displaystyle\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell_{1}

and
\displaystyle\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell_{1}\;\_
. We then use
Collapse-Dup-MutBorrow

to simplify
\displaystyle\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell_{0}\;\_
and
\displaystyle\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell_{0}\;\_

into
\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell_{0}\;\_
. We get the following environment, which is a fixed-point.

\displaystyle l_{0}\mapsto\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell_{0},

\displaystyle A_{2}\,\{\,\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell_{0}\;\_,\,\mathsf{loan}^{m}\,\ell_{2}\,\}

\displaystyle l\mapsto\mathsf{borrow}^{m}\,\ell_{2}\;\sigma_{3}
